ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u5	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to the High House an analysis of the implementation of the state budget and monetary policy objectives in 2020 together with the opinion of the College of the Supreme Audit Chamber on discharge for the Council of Ministers for 2020. In view of the very short time for my speech, I will only pass on the most important thesis presented in this document. In 2020 a wide range of solutions have been applied to a much greater extent than before, which, although lawful, have, however, distorted the transparency and efficiency of the implementation of the state budget. That is why I will pay the most attention to this issue. High Seym! For the first time, we have assessed the descriptive implementation of the budget law for 2020. Although the vast majority of individual controls gave a positive assessment, their number in the structure of the evaluations decreased by almost 4 percentage points compared to the previous year. At the same time, on a similar scale, the number of evaluations in descriptive form increased, which this year we formulated 47. We have also issued one negative assessment and in one case we have waived the evaluation. [[So that's it.]] The Supreme Audit Chamber states that the state budget and the budget of European funds have been implemented in accordance with the Budget Law for 2020. [[Yeah, it is.]] All the limits laid down directly in this Act concerning expenditure and expenditure, budget deficits, debt and guarantees and guarantees of the State Treasury were respected. However, a significant proportion of the operations related to the financing of the prevention, prevention and eradication of the COVID-19 epidemic were excluded from these limits. The Council of Ministers' report on the implementation of the State budget for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2020 was drawn up on the basis of the data included in the reports of the individual State budget managers and the applicable legal regulations. The data contained in the audited accounts reflected the nature of the operations carried out. At this point, however, I must stress that by applying mechanisms to reduce budget transparency in 2020 this report does not reflect all operations affecting the state of the government sector's finances, and not just those related to the prevention, prevention and fight against COVID-19. High Seym! Initially, the budget law for 2020 plans to balance the revenue and expenditure of the State budget. This was possible thanks to the introduction of a number of legal solutions which enabled public expenditure of PLN 20.7 billion to be financed from non-budget funds. The deterioration during the financial year of the conditions for the implementation of the State budget due to the development of the COVID-19 epidemic was the reason for the revision of the budget law, which took place on 28 October 2020. As a result of the amendment, an update of the basic macroeconomic parameters and budgetary volumes was made, in particular, a deficit of PLN 109.3 billion was set in place of the previously anticipated balance. High Seym! As I have already pointed out, major financial operations on important public tasks were planned and carried out in 2020 with the exception of the expenditure account and the government budget deficit. It also used operations which allowed State budget expenditure to be shown in different periods than they were actually incurred. An example of operations carried out without the State budget was the free transfer of treasury securities of PLN 18.3 billion to entities authorised under other laws than the budget law. This included, among others, the following: universities, infrastructure companies, Polish TV and Polish Radio, Polish Development Fund and Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. Another example was the granting of interest-free loans to the Solidarity Fund of PLN 15.5 billion, which had already had past loans liabilities of nearly PLN 13 billion. Most of the expenditure earmarked for the prevention, prevention and eradication of the COVID-19 epidemic has also been financed without the State budget. From the state budget and the budget of European funds, only PLN 23.3 billion were allocated for these purposes, thus less than 4%. the total expenditure of those budgets. On the other hand, the Budgetary Act does not include funds of PLN 166.1 billion collected by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego for the Anti-Action Fund COVID-19 and by the Polish Development Fund SA. The issue of bonds related to the COVID-19 epidemic resulting in an increase in the State's liabilities did not increase the value of sovereign public debt calculated in accordance with national methodology. Solution used to avoid exceeding the prudential threshold of 55% gross domestic product increases the costs borne by the wider public sector, as the operation of the commitments undertaken by the Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego and the Polish Development Fund requires higher costs than the handling of debt incurred directly by the State Treasury. The repayment of commitments entered into by these entities will in the end most likely be borne by the State Treasury. The financing of financial and crisis shields in a form that is not shown in debt calculated according to the national methodology but only according to the Union methodology resulted in the highest gap between general government debt and public government debt at the end of 2020. This difference has increased more than four times compared to previous years and reached PLN 224 billion, or close to 10% Gross domestic product and 20,2% State public debt. The operations which have allowed State budget expenditure to be shown in a different period than will actually be incurred include State budget expenditure of over PLN 41 billion which was transferred in 2020 to the Solidarity Fund accounts, the COVID-19 Anti-Action Fund and the Government Road Development Fund, and have remained unused until the end of the year. The operations formally increasing the deficit of the state budget in 2020 also included non-extinguishable expenditure. In 2019 this type of expenditure was set at less than PLN 140 million, while in 2020 it was already close to PLN 12 billion. Taking into account the deadline set for the completion of their implementation on 30 November 2021, this de facto means that two budgets are being implemented this year. [[And that's a scandal.]] Such an important scale of unexploited expenditure also limits control... For the second time, I would like to ask you very warmly to allow me to speak. ...parliamentary, as during the period when the implementation of the State budget is discussed in 2020, the expenditure allocated to this year may still be implemented for many months. High Seym! The application of the operations in question, although based on legislation, reduces the transparency of public finances and also reduces the effectiveness of other provisions, in particular those relating to the limit on public expenditure or public debt. Following the above findings, the Supreme Audit Chamber points out the need to plan expenditure on important public tasks in the Budget Act and in particular in the State budget. It is also reasonable to discuss the differences in the presentation of revenue, expenditure, deficit and public debt in two different ways, i.e. according to national and EU methodologies. High Seym! The remaining irregularities identified by the Supreme Audit Chamber relating to the implementation of the budget in 2020 concerned primarily the non-targeted and uneconomic spending of funds, the failure to grant and use grants, and errors in accounting records and budgetary reports, and therefore the recurring issues each year. The detailed results of the audit were presented in separate information on the results of the inspections submitted to you by your marshals and the parliamentary committees in mid-June this year. The Supreme Audit Chamber welcomes the implementation of monetary policy in 2020. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! The College of the Supreme Audit Chamber, following an analysis of the implementation of the state budget and monetary policy objectives in 2020, adopted a resolution on 9 June 2021 which expressed a positive opinion on discharge for the Council of Ministers for 2020, but also indicated the need for action to increase the transparency of public finances. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u8	Thank you very much, sir. I ask the President of the National Bank of Poland, Mr Adam Glapiński, to present a report on the activities of the National Bank of Poland in 2020.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u9	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Messengers, Dear Members, As President of the National Bank of Poland and Chairman of the Monetary Policy Council, I have the honour to present a report on the implementation of monetary policy objectives for 2020 and a report on the activities of the National Bank of Poland in 2020. Of course, due to the introduction of a time limit, I will only discuss the main elements of this report. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The outbreak of the pandemic last year led to the biggest collapse of economic activity worldwide in decades. In order to mitigate the effects of the pandemic in many countries, governments and central banks have taken unprecedented anti-crisis measures. In Poland too, the pandemic and its restrictions led to a strong decline in economic activity last year. We were threatened not only by a very deep recession, but also by permanent reduction of the potential of the Polish economy. We were threatened by the bankruptcy of many companies, a strong rise in unemployment and a decline in Poles' income for a long time. In addition, inflation would fall below the target of the National Bank of Poland in the medium term. Therefore, the National Bank of Poland as one of the first central banks in Europe, including in the world, has implemented actions aimed at supporting the Polish economy in the face of a pandemic. Firstly, in 2020 we tripled the interest rates, including the reference rate by a total of 1,4 percentage points, to a level of 0.1%. Secondly, we have launched structural open market operations to buy tax bonds on the secondary market and guaranteed by the Treasury. As part of these operations by the end of June 2021, we have accumulated bonds worth almost PLN 136 billion, for the first time in the history of the Polish Central Bank. Thirdly, we have lowered the minimum reserve rate. Fourthly, we have provided bank loans and finally, in the face of strong appreciation of the gold, fast and strong, the National Bank of Poland has intervened in the currency market, strengthening the impact of the earlier loosening of monetary policy. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! What are the effects of our policy today, which we implemented last year? There is no doubt that NBP's activities reduced the costs of pandemics for the Polish economy, for Polish companies, and above all for all Poles and Poles. Thanks to our decisions, the interest rate on loans has fallen significantly. As a result, household and business spending on borrowing decreased by PLN 7 billion per year. Moreover, the profitability of the Treasury bonds and those guaranteed by the Treasury decreased significantly and the liquidity of the secondary market of those bonds was maintained. This allowed for the immediate mobilisation of payments to Polish companies and households under government crisis shields. In total, about PLN 140 billion has been paid out since the start of the pandemic. As a result, the decline in real GDP last year was only 2.7% in Poland and was more than twice as small as the average decrease in the European Union. Moreover, unemployment has not increased significantly, making it one of the lowest in the European Union today. At the same time, the financial results of most companies are very good and the real income of Poles is growing all the time. Most importantly, all forecasts show that not only this year, but also in the next few years we have a chance of very strong economic growth. It is worth noting that the NBP's activities not only effectively supported the Polish economy, including its current recovery, but also significantly reduced the cost of anti-crisis programmes for the budget and thus for the Polish taxpayer. The savings of the state budget for this purpose are estimated at over PLN 7 billion, only last year, which I am talking about. In addition, in 2020 The NBP provided the state budget with PLN 7.4 billion from the profit generated. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In the last months of this year, concerns about the condition of the Polish economy have resolved in many comments the concern for the increase in inflation. The inflation has actually increased, which is obviously the subject of careful analysis by the NBP, but the increased inflation rate is a temporary one, and for its significant increase in the last six months, which, moreover, relates to many economies around the world, is mainly due to factors which are wholly independent of NBP's monetary policy. The annual price dynamics increase primarily the global increase in raw material prices, which is strongly strengthened by the so-called. the statistical base effect, because we compare everything with what happened in such a period a year earlier. Plus, we're still experiencing the effects of pandemic disruptions in international transport and global supply chains. Regulatory factors have also contributed significantly to price increases in Poland, including a significant increase in trash export charges and an increase in electricity prices as a result of the EU's CO2 rationing policy. I would like to stress strongly that these factors are completely independent of any NBP action and also from the national economic situation. They are responsible, as I stress, entirely for exceeding the NBP's inflation target. If we didn't take them into account, we'd be below the middle as far as the inflation target is concerned. Forecasts indicate that inflation is likely to remain above the upper limit of the inflation target as a result of these factors by the end of this year, but will decrease by next year. I would like to remind you that, in line with the monetary policy strategy pursued, the objective of the NBP is to maintain inflation at 2.5%. with a symmetrical variation interval of 1 percentage point in the medium term. And that's what we're doing. In the longer term, inflation remains at a level consistent with the NBP's objective. And that's what it'll be like in the next few years. During the period during which I am privileged to be president of NBP, inflation in Poland was on average 2.1% so it was fully in line with the inflation target. In the following years, unless further external shocks occur, inflation will return to NBP target level. We will continue to conduct monetary policy that supports sustainable economic growth and long-term economic development of Poland. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The COVID-19 Pandemic has created significant risks not only for economic growth and macroeconomic equilibrium but also for the stability of the financial system as a whole. The National Bank of Poland, in cooperation with the government and other institutions of the financial security network, has taken decisive action to reduce the risk in this area. First of all, the NBP quickly provided liquidity to the banking sector. This was mainly served by the structural open market operations already mentioned by me and by lowering the minimum reserve rate. The good liquidity situation of the banking sector is confirmed not only by the liquidity ratios at the moment, but also by the fact that, during this difficult period, no liquidity support was necessary for any bank in the form of a refinancing loan throughout the crisis. In turn, the reduction in NBP's interest rates was beneficial for maintaining the dynamics of the economy and contributed to reducing the burden on existing borrowers. With support from financial shields and moratoria, which you remember, which have been introduced - the moratoria of repayment of loans - this reduced the risk of a strong deterioration in the quality of the bank's credit portfolio. Banks have significantly increased their prudential write-downs on credit risk, but their actual credit losses have so far been less than we feared at the beginning of the pandemic. All you have to do is reach out to the media. The aim of reducing the risk of excessive credit reduction by banks was to enable them to use funds from the so-called funds. a systemic risk buffer. In March 2020, the liquidation of the buffer was recommended by the Finance Minister of the Financial Stability Committee - a macro-prudential body, which I chair as President of the NBP. As a result, banks have received more than PLN 30 billion of capital, which has so far been frozen in their balance sheets. At the same time, we have stated that the funds released from the buffer cannot increase the planned dividends, but should be allocated to cover losses and credit to the economy. The banking sector ended in 2020 with a solvency ratio of more than 20%, and by more than 1.3 percentage points more than in 2019. The solvency ratio increased, did not decrease. This was precisely the result of the bank's retention of profits. In conclusion, despite the scale of shock and high uncertainty regarding the consequences of the Covid-19 pandemic, the entire crisis in the face of the counter-measures it has taken has proved to be a less threat to the stability of the financial system than it seemed last year. Today, high write-downs to the legal risks of foreign exchange loans provided by banks are more and more likely to pose a threat. The NBP received a request from the Supreme Court on this matter and the reply of the National Bank of Poland has already been forwarded. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! As a central bank of the State, the National Bank of Poland is responsible for carrying out issue activities and organizing cash settlements. This task was particularly important last year, as the country's epidemic situation has resulted in a significant increase in the demand of Poles for cash. Significant, violent, and fast. Under these difficult conditions, NBP met the logistical challenges, ensuring a smooth and continuous supply of Polish currency to the banks. Thanks to this, Poles and Poles had undisturbed access to cash through ATMs and bank facilities, which maintained public confidence in the state and its institutions. These experiences show that in crisis situations cash continues to play a very important role in stabilising the financial system. However, during the pandemic, we noted cases of refusal to accept cash. In the opinion of the National Bank of Poland, this situation is legally and socially unacceptable. Therefore, in order to safeguard the universal availability and acceptance of cash in the payment market in November 2020, I announced the launch of work on the development of a national cash market security strategy. The preparation of solutions to ensure the wide-ranging security of cash trading, belonging to the NBP's priority tasks, in 2021, has now been entrusted to the Board for cash trading at the NBP Management Board. It required additional cooperation with all public institutions competent for the areas covered by the strategy and representatives of the cash market. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The last year was also full of challenges for the foreign exchange reserve management process. We had to face not only the increase in volatility in the financial markets (the bell), the deterioration of the credit standing of issuers of securities under conditions of severe economic downturn, but also the increase in operational and clearing risk due to the transition of many institutions in the sector to remote work. I am proud to say that in this area the NBP has also fulfilled its statutory tasks without reservation. At the end of 2020, the official reserve assets of the National Bank of Poland accounted for almost EUR 126 billion, of which the monetary gold stock of almost 230 t is an important part. The monetary gold stock has increased to 230 t. This size of reserves allowed to maintain the commonly used reserve adequacy indicators at the appropriate level, strengthening the financial credibility of Poland on foreign markets and securing potential balance of payments needs. Of course, the high security of the funds invested and their proper high liquidity are required above all, and only after these conditions have been met can we take care to increase the profitability of the reserves over the long term. The various measures taken have reduced the risk of the reserve portfolio but also increased its profitability. In 2020 the rate of return on reserves calculated in PLN was 5.2% , calculated in the currency of the instruments - 2,3% And it was much higher than the average in the last five years. As a result, the income from investment activities related to foreign exchange reserve management amounted to PLN 6.9 billion and was the main component of the financial result, which amounted to PLN 9.3 billion in 2020. According to the NBP Act 95% This amount has already been paid to the State budget. Given all this, I am proud to say today that in 2020, as in previous years, NBP not only achieved its statutory goals but also actively supported the Polish economy in the face of pandemics. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u10	Thank you very much to the President of the National Bank of Poland. I now ask Mr Andrzej Nobletta to present a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u11	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. President! Ministers! I have the honour to present my position on the report on the activities of the National Bank of Poland in 2020 together with the position of the Committee on Public Finance. The report covers the implementation by the National Bank of Poland as the central bank of the state of statutory tasks in 2020 in the areas of monetary policy, actions for the stability of the financial system, emission activity, foreign exchange reserve management, foreign exchange activities, actions for the payment system, treasury management, analytical and research activities, statistical, educational and information activities and international cooperation. The report also takes into account the legislative, internal and financial situation of the National Bank of Poland as at 31 December 2020. The reporting year covers the implementation of the tasks of the National Bank of Poland under the conditions of the COVID-19 pandemic, which were appropriate to the related challenges faced by the Polish economy. The main objective of monetary policy in 2020 was to maintain price stability while promoting sustainable economic growth and the stability of the financial system. In 2020 due to the outbreak of the world pandemic and economic downturn, as well as the expected reduction in inflation, the National Bank of Poland significantly loosened monetary policy. The Monetary Policy Council reduced NBP interest rates, including from 1,5% up to 0,1% , as well as a minimum reserve rate of 3.5% Up to 0.5%. The National Bank of Poland also conducted structural open market operations involving the purchase on the secondary market of treasury securities and debt securities guaranteed by the Treasury. The loosening of monetary policy has limited the scale of the decline in economic activity and the cost of pandemics in the labour market. The decline in real GDP in Poland was more than twice as low as on average in the European Union and the unemployment rate was among the lowest in Europe at the end of 2020. At the same time, the conducted monetary policy ensured the core task of the National Bank of Poland, namely keeping inflation at a level consistent with the NBP's inflation objective, i.e. 2,5% plus minus 1 percentage point. in the medium term. The inflation of consumer goods and services in December 2020 was 2.4% year-to-year and year-to-year average 3.4%. It should be noted that the activities of the National Bank of Poland, through a positive impact on the financial situation of borrowers, reduced the credit risk, whose growth was the most important channel for the impact of pandemics on the stability of the banking system. Managing reserves, the National Bank of Poland sought to complement and ensure the necessary liquidity of the funds invested. The income from investment activities related to the management of foreign exchange reserves without taking into account the realised and unrealised exchange rate differences in 2020 amounted, as the President mentioned, to PLN 6.9 billion, and in the previous year, in 2019 it amounted to PLN 7.6 billion under different conditions. The state banking service operated by the National Bank of Poland has contributed to ensuring the security and fluidity of the settlement of the funds of the public finance sector. As at 31 December, NBP kept 25,719 accounts for 2953 customers. The National Bank of Poland was involved in the operation of the European temporary support instrument SURE and served the so-called. a joint account for the collection of tax and non-tax liabilities of the State budget. This appropriation is The National Bank of Poland implemented monetary policy in line with monetary policy objectives for 2020. In view of the shock from the pandemic, it was necessary to use instruments which were not used or used only in exceptional situations in recent years, i.e. the use of unconditional collection of bonds. The Public Finance Commission took note of the report on the activities of the National Bank of Poland in 2020 at its meeting on 14 July 2021. She listened to the extensive discussion of this report by your NBP vice-president and held a detailed discussion of the report, listened to explanations and answers to questions asked by Members. The report of the Committee on Public Finance is contained in print No 1391. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

In the case of extra-zone investments, these results were much weaker. In 2020 the number of decisions was 372, i.e. 18 more than the year before. This document contains information on the functioning of special economic zones as at 31 December 2020 and covers, as in previous years' reports, the following issues: first, the location and degree of development of each zone; second, the effects of the zones taking into account the number of permits granted, the number of jobs created, the values created, the industry structure, the investments made and the countries of origin of capital; thirdly, the activities of zone management companies. It is worth noting here that in 2020 the total area of zones remained unchanged and amounted to 22 949 ha. On the other hand, the average degree of zoning has increased to almost 67%. At the end of 2020, entrepreneurs had 2289 valid business permits in the zones. In 2020 the accumulated value of the invested capital amounted to nearly PLN 145.9 billion, which meant an increase of almost 13.9 billion, i.e. almost 10.5 percent. in relation to the previous year. Capital invested in less than 62% of zones came from five countries: Poland, Germany, the Netherlands, Luxembourg and the United States. The industrial structure of the investment has not changed significantly. The same sectors of activity occupy the first three positions. The largest share in the cumulative value of the investment was by entrepreneurs from the automotive industry - almost 27%. The following places were taken by producers of rubber and plastic products - less than 9%, and metal products - about 7%. The largest industrial concentration was characterized by the Katowice zone. In 2020, 56.7% of the leading industry in the zone fell. Traditionally, the Legnica zone also had a high share of the dominant industry. In the Wałbrzych, pole and suwałka zones, the share of the dominant industry was also high, exceeding 40% the value of the investment. In the Legnica, Katowice and Wałbrzych zones, the dominant industry was automotive industry, and in the pole and suwałska wood processing industry. In 2020 companies managing special economic zones allocated about PLN 5.6 million to the promotion. On the other hand, 286 million PLN was spent on building infrastructure in the area of zones, with the expenditure of management companies accounting for 18% , the remaining parts of the expenditure were financed by municipalities, media gestors and the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u207	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I will briefly address all the comments that have been made, because I do feel that in this discussion we do not understand the legal situation in which we are moving today. Perhaps the question of Mr Zandberg's philosophical attention first. Do we need special economic zones today, and what is the scale, how wide is the range of public aid granted under not only the Special Economic Zones Act, but also the Polish Investment Zone regulations or other legal solutions? Where is this public aid offered and is it needed? After all, as the Member said - and it is difficult to disagree with it - we already have quite good infrastructure, outstanding staff and should rather invest in public policies, public services, rather than subsidising and, in one way or another, favoring entrepreneurs. I'd say it's a fake alternative, you can't say either-or. You have to say, and-and, and so this, and this is what you need. There is a need for obvious investment in public services, but there is also a need to maintain such incentives, because today we are taking part in real global competition and are also competing with what kind of offer, such as an attractive offer, we can present to attractive investors. This level of attractiveness of the investor is present today in the Act on the Polish Investment Zone, because today, unlike the 1994 Act, when, in fact, let's remember, every workplace was at the weight of gold, we are in different conditions, we want to attract high-value investments, such that will provide highly paid jobs that will allow us to build a value chain with local R &amp; D centres that will ensure attractive work for our very good people. Why is this necessary? For example, because - and here I will appeal to the doubts put forward by Mr Rosati, who said that this is an obsolete instrument. Yes, privileged areas have their history actually as long as trade exists. In Europe, probably the first such privileged zone became a free port in Gibraltar in the 18th century. And these are even free ports: Bremen, Hamburg. This is the history of privileged areas. More importantly, the privileged areas are offered by all countries in the region: Czechs, Slovaks, Hungarians, Baltic countries. And we today, competing, cannot deprive ourselves of this competitive advantage. It is hard to imagine today that we will make attractive investments without a similar instrument. Thus, with regard to this capital, referring also to the questions of the Members, how much and how we balance, how we calculate the rate of return of tax advantages granted versus benefits, the answer is not simple, because of course this reply cannot only cover the number of jobs and the amount of tax paid by these operators, because we should take into account the taxes on jobs created, as well as the products and services produced and the value of exports. So this balance should actually be done and presented - this is a valuable point - and as the occasion will be, I will certainly undertake this task. I will complete the Minister's reply on the scale of the aid, because there has been a question raised that this zone does not want to serve and that the Łódź zone offers worse conditions. As an investment zone, we have Poland as a whole today, but the scale of public aid is defined by a map of public aid approved by the European Commission. Thus, the fact that one zone offers more aid and another smaller one is not the result of a decision by the board of directors of one or another zone. The Podkarpackie area provides the largest public aid, as this is the result of the aid map, and the aid map is dictated by macroeconomic factors linked to unemployment, population density, with dozens of factors that make it possible for this public aid to be larger and smaller in the area concerned. Therefore, it is not a question of the fact that the Mielec administrative zone near Wałbrzych will provide the assistance it can provide in Podkarpacie. No, it will provide such assistance as is appropriate to the area covered by the public aid. You asked a question about what workers work there. It is worth noting not only what the Minister said, that these are jobs, employment contracts, because of this they are accounted for by investors using the aid, but also that they are mostly highly paid jobs, first of all, and second of all, 90% of them are Polish workers, also in the case of managers, and this is also an evolutionary change that has occurred since 1994 until today, when really at every level, including these managers, Polish workers are employed. There was a deep misunderstanding in the discussion of undeveloped land. Indeed, we are talking about the development of zones on the basis of the 1994 Act at 67% , but many times in this Chamber we have pointed out - also I, by presenting a report on this law - and the local Members, the regional Members know this very well, that many times land has been brought in which they do not have the right to become investment land because they are either under high voltage lines or in flood areas. Why were they brought in anyway? Not because an investor made a deal, just because a local bogeyman wanted to report in another election campaign that he had a chance to get an investment, because one or another area was included in the zone. Nor does this inclusion involve, ladies and gentlemen, the resources necessary to be borne by the local authorities. Investment in the field of land weapons is carried out by zone companies, so it is a multiple advantage for local governments. One last remark. You've already touched the Minister. It's about Konin. Let us remind you that at the top of the pandemic the biggest investment in Poland was carried out in the vicinity of Konin. Johnson Matthew is a British battery company. Their state-of-the-art factory is currently being built in a special zone near Konin. It's worth remembering. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u211	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on the Economy, I present a report on the Government's draft law on specific solutions related to the special use of forest land. The project is contained in print No. 1394, and the report of the joint committees in print No. 1415. High Izbo! The aim of the project is to address land acquisition for investments in the implementation of State policies on energy, electromobility or transport, to disseminate new technologies, to improve air quality and to invest justified by the need for strategic production for state defence, high electronic technologies and processors, innovative hydrogen technology, aviation and automotive and the plastics industry. The solutions contained in this project will allow, within 2 years of the entry into force of the Act, the conversion of forests, land and other properties remaining on the Management Board of the State Forest Forests Forest Farm for the purpose of localising investments in the above mentioned. range. In addition, the possibility of replacing other properties of the State Treasury adjacent to the above mentioned is envisaged. the property of the State Forests, if necessary for the fulfilment of the objectives set out in the Act. High Izbo! To date, in accordance with the Article. 38e of the Forest Law of 28 September 1991, such a conversion was possible only if the forest management objectives were met after the land was assessed by the eligible property value estimates. Since the conversion is to be subject to land of special value, the draft law contains a number of restrictions to ensure that this instrument is used only in a situation of absolute necessity. Namely, the rules will only apply for two years, and the catalogue of investments to be replaced is set out in the annex to the Act. In addition, the Annex sets out the list of registration parcels to be converted. It is 25 plots in Stalowa Wola and 55 plots in Jaworzno. In addition, land covered by the nature conservation forms referred to in Article will not be subject to conversion. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 point 1 and 5 of the Nature Conservation Act of 16 April 2004, i.e. land in national parks, nature reserves, landscape parks, protected landscape areas and Natura 2000 areas. The project includes the condition that State Forest Farm will receive only land on which forest management is possible as a result of the conversion. Entities which will be able to conclude swap agreements with State Forest Forests are, first, state legal entities referred to in Article. 3 (1) (a) The Law of 16 December 2016 on the rules for the management of state property, second, legal persons for which the Treasury holds the majority of shares within the meaning of Article. 2 point 2 of that Act, thirdly, local government units. In addition, it will be necessary to obtain the opinion of the Sejm Committee responsible for forestry and forestry management on the conversion and transfer of land ownership. Another restriction I would like to mention is the introduction in the article. 5 the provision that, in the event of non-investment within 10 years of the date of the conversion of the State Forest Farm, the State Forests shall have the right to claim reimbursement of the immovable property or to pay the difference in value of the immovable property which is the subject of the conversion. In addition, conversions in accordance with Article. 2 will be performed by the General Director of the State Forests. Like I said, we only have two locations listed in the annex. There is no need to cedit it on the strings, which is around 430 in Poland. The replacement shall be carried out by the Director-General of the State Forests upon request by the Minister responsible for the Environment in agreement with the Minister responsible for Climate Affairs and the Minister responsible for Economic Affairs after consulting the competent Sejm Committee. High Izbo! As regards the issue of the replacement of neighbouring immovable property referred to in Article. 1 paragraph 3 of the project will carry out it in accordance with Article. 11 Act on Real Estate Management The competent authority - most often it is a senior performing tasks in the field of government administration - taking into account, of course, the exceptions resulting from the Real Estate Management Act and separate laws. The replacement shall be carried out at the request of the Minister responsible for the environment as set out in paragraph 1. 1, in agreement with the Minister responsible for Climate Affairs and the Minister responsible for Economic Affairs, and also after consulting the competent Sejm Committee, in the same way as in the case of conversions by the State Forests. High Izbo! Art. 4 of the project states that the State Forest Forests on the land it manages will carry out a free-of-charge cut-off and clean-up of the trees and shrubs within the deadline set with the other party of the swap agreement. The wood thus obtained becomes free of charge the property of the State Forests. Ladies and gentlemen! During the work of the combined economic and environmental committees, the problem of taking part in the exchange procedure between the forest management and forestry committees was very heated by the debaters. The question of participation in the procedure, which of course raises numerous comments, is dictated by the aim of an open, transparent procedure for converting parcels into investments that will become an economic flywheel. We are talking here about two locations: in Stalowa Wola, i.e. in Podkarpacie, and in Silesia in Jaworzno. Let us remind you that Silesia is experiencing a very difficult period of transformation, resulting from the need to move away from coal in the energy generation process. This is, ladies and gentlemen, a multi-annual process, which you know very well, especially, I think, Members of Silesia. This is a process combined with the need to reclassify very many groups of employees. Of course, part of it will be that they will go away to deserved pensions, but many will have to give a new source of living, so any investment that can create jobs is simply desirable there. Ladies and gentlemen, it's not like investors are pushing their doors and windows. We are talking about the situation that took place last year, when in competition with Hungary we lost the opportunity to bring an investor, i.e. BMW factory to Poland. Finally, it was in Hungary because it was not immediately to use the area of 600 ha, which could be used for this investment. Ladies and gentlemen, it is so that a potential investor chooses different locations in Central and Eastern Europe as from the menu card. If we are not ready as a country with areas invested, armed, where investment can be started immediately, we will lose in these competitions. Now, a little bit about the committee procedure. High Izbo! During the work of the Joint Economic and Environmental Committees, it was decided in a vote to proceed immediately to the project process. These include mainly state policies to support, develop and implement projects related to energy, electromobility or transport, to promote and develop new technologies and to improve air quality, as well as state policy on strategic production for state defence, implementation of high electronic technologies and electromobility processes, innovative hydrogen technology, aviation production, automotive and plastics industries. The point is that if, within 10 years from the date of the replacement, the objective set out in the replacement agreement is not met, the entity referred to in Article. 2 (2) 1 or 2, may require the return of the property from any owner. The conditions for such reimbursement are set out in Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1, 2, 3 and 4. An additional security for the property of the State Treasury referred to in the draft law is the provision in the article. 6, which states that each transfer of ownership of immovable property referred to in Article. 1 paragraph 1, requires a positive opinion from the Sejm Committee responsible for forestry and forestry management within one month of the date of service of the request for an opinion. In addition, the replacement of the property concerned by the draft law may be carried out within 2 years of the date of entry into force of the law, as evidenced by the provision in the article. 7. Draft Print Act No 1394 allows the implementation of very important social and economic investments, which will generate economic development and contribute to the acquisition of many thousands of new jobs in the era of socio-economic transformation, the transformation of the raw materials and energy sector, due to the adoption of many regulations of European Union law in Poland, especially as we are in the process of very broad implementation of the plan, especially in areas where the raw materials and energy sector is undergoing very large changes. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u303	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the report of the Infrastructure Commission on the Government draft law amending the Rail Transport Act, print No 1393. The purpose of this law is to introduce state examinations into the driving position. Currently, both driver training and examination are carried out by the same bodies: training and examination centres, which are not public institutions. The draft law introduces major changes related to the entitlement of drivers. According to the project, training of train drivers candidates will be entrusted to training centres that will be created from transformed training and examination centres. In turn, the exam itself will belong, as in the case of road vehicle drivers, to the state institution - in this case to the President of the Railway Transport Office. The examination will be carried out at the premises of the Railway Transport Office or in one of its field departments. The driver's licence test will be a theoretical test conducted in the form of a test. It should be noted that a candidate for a driver must obtain one more document, in addition to the driver's licence already in possession under European Union law, before being admitted to work. driver's certificate as the driver's licence itself does not entitle the driver to drive the train. The draft law provides for a similar solution for driver's certificates as for obtaining a licence, namely training centres which will be created from transformed training and examination centres. In turn, the exam itself will belong to the President of the UTK. In addition to the theoretical part of the test, as well as the practical part of driving along the train track in the driver's cab, the test with the president of the UTK will be enriched by the practical part carried out in the driving simulator. The part of the exam carried out using the simulator will be designed to check the response to emergency and dangerous situations. The examination will be carried out by examiners employed by the President of the UTK. Given that training centres will continue to be responsible for training and certification during the practical part, i.e. practical driving, in the driver's cab next to the UTK examiner there will be an additional examiner from the training centre itself holding driving licences for the type of railway vehicle in which the test is carried out. It is planned that the training centre will carry out an internal examination of the candidate from the knowledge of the type of railway vehicle and the railway infrastructure in which the examination will be carried out before admission to the State examination. It should be stressed that the essential objective of the Act is to increase the safety of rail traffic by ensuring a uniform and reliable testing system for train drivers. The draft law was introduced by the Council of Ministers on 13 July 2021, print No 1393. Its first reading took place at the meeting of the Infrastructure Committee yesterday. In the vote, all Members supported this proposal unanimously. Therefore, Mr. Marshal, I ask the High House to adopt this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u308	Thank you, Minister. Mrs Karolin Pavliczak, Parliamentary Club Left. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u309	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Rail transport is the second most important road transport sector in the land transport sector, and security is one of the most important issues. In the near future, the railroad will face important challenges. The implementation of the European Rail Traffic Management System will lead to significant changes in the work of train drivers and on-call traffic, and the automation of rail work is gaining momentum, resulting in the need for training on new skills. The draft law amending the Rail Transport Act is therefore intended to introduce state tests for the driver's position. I do not really know why it took so long that drivers, as the only professional group responsible for driving, had not yet taken the State exam. The new solution will objectively assess both the skills and the quality of training for drivers. The Act introduces a very important monitoring system for drivers in the field of their professional competence. The introduction of state exams for this position is one of the elements of the comprehensive operation of the President of the Railway Transport Office, hereinafter referred to as the President of the UTK, aimed at ensuring a safe and competitive rail transport market in Poland. The solutions introduced are the result of detailed analyses of European Union legislation. The bill also provides for the creation of a central register of drivers, which collects consistent and reliable data in one place, distributed so far in different databases. It introduces provisions allowing access to the employee's data collected in the register. The collection and exchange of this information between the operators responsible for ensuring the safety of rail transport is a necessary and proportionate measure to ensure the effective management of the identified risks relating to the personnel of drivers and drivers of railway vehicles. It is precisely the lack of possibility for railway operators and infrastructure managers to exchange information on personnel performing critical to safety operations that poses a threat to the safety of the railway system, e.g. by allowing people who are tired or who do not meet other requirements to work. So, as you can see, many of these changes introduce favourable solutions for the safety of rail transport in our country, but this project - and it is worth mentioning here - does not contain an extremely important, even fundamental issue, having a direct impact on the safety of rail traffic, which is the central register of working time. There are no real tools to control the working time of drivers today, and unfortunately the Labour Code does not fully secure this. The creation of a central time management system for drivers and the improvement of the train training system would be a coherent, good step towards the efficient management of human resources. In view of the importance of the proposed amendments, the draft should also be the subject of the work of the Task Force. Economic policy and the labour market, however, has run out of time. The trade union organisations that have asked us on this issue also asked the Railway Transport Office to urgently restore the appropriate consultation procedure by the Social Dialogue Council, taking into account the statutory time limits for the opinion of such important draft laws and enabling the RDS to present a position that will be taken into account in the legislative work. In view of the above, on behalf of the Left Club, I submit an amendment to this project, which will guarantee such a record of working time. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

During the reporting period, the Board took up inter alia. issues related to repeated attacks of aggression against public media journalists and met with the Boards of Polish Television, Polish Radio, Polish Press Agency and Auditorium on the operation of companies during the SARS-CoV-2 epidemic. During the reporting period, the Council adopted 33 resolutions on the formation of personal storage in public broadcasting companies and on the issue of the state of finances. The Council took its control powers under the Act on the Council of National Media and the Act on Radio and Television and the Act on the Polish Press Agency on an ongoing basis. The Management Boards of Public Radio and Television Companies shall periodically submit to the Council the documents set out in the Act which serve members of the Board to assess the activities of public radio and television companies' bodies and the State Press Agency SA. The work undertaken in 2020 by the National Radio and Television Council was carried out on the basis of the objectives adopted in the regulatory strategy for the period 2017-2022. The National Radio and Television Council and the orientations set in the task budget of the National Radio and Television Council, approved in the parliamentary procedure. The National Radio and Television Council participated, among others. in the legislative work concerning the introduction of digital radio and television in Poland, the establishment of programming and financial plans and payment cards for public broadcasters, the distribution of revenues from subscription and compensation, the organisation of researches on the content and reception of media services and the implementation of the Telemetry Poland project consisting of the launch of a single source measurement of electronic media consumption. It was important not only for the organisation, but also for the performance of public tasks that the founding body - President of the National Radio and Television Council - appointed the National Media Institute - was set up on 31 December 2020. To the state budget for the activities of the National Radio and Television Council, revenues of over PLN 35 million were distributed, for which among others. revenue from concession fees of more than PLN 27 million. In 2020, the President of the National Radio and Television Council issued more than 74 314 administrative decisions, including 74,090 subscription decisions. The National Radio and Television Council distributed 650 million subscription receipts and treasury securities with a nominal value of PLN 1 million, to carry out missionary tasks by public broadcasters. [[Voice from the room: Thousand.]] Yeah, 1950 grand. zł. The board received 33 450 applications from subscribers for subscription arrears. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the position expressed by the Committee on Culture and Media and welcomes the actions taken by the National Radio and Television Council as well as the National Media Council. The Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice will vote in favour of the Council's report last year. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u421	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present a government bill amending the law - Civil Code and the Act - Code of Civil Procedure, print No. 1344. The proposed law provides for solutions to improve and clarify the rules in the Civil Code on the institution of exploitation, the liability of adult tenants' children for the obligations of parents under the lease agreement, the interruption and suspension of the limitation period as a result of the initiation of mediation or the submission of a request for a settlement attempt. The draft also provides for the repeal of the Article. 159 Civil Code. As regards the institution involved in the course of the limitation period, we propose an amendment to the Article. 121 Civil Code. The proposed amendment will eliminate the practice for creditors to apply for a settlement attempt only in order to interrupt the limitation period. This will ensure that the procedural situation of the parties to the dispute is clarified and that the request for a settlement attempt is restored to the original appropriate objective of concluding the settlement, as is the case with requests for mediation proceedings. The effect of this amendment is also to amend the Article. 124 § 2 Civil Code by eliminating in this provision the reference to mediation. We are also designing a change in the scope of art. 183 In fact, these changes are intended to direct the parties towards a genuine settlement of conflicts and a settlement process. The draft also provides for the repeal of the Article. 159 Civil Code. We consider that the current content of this provision, excluding the form of a notarial act in situations where land is contributed as a contribution to an agricultural production cooperative, is not justified. Maintaining this exception does not find any reasonable justification, it seems pointless, and the repeal of this regulation in our assessment will contribute to ensuring the certainty of the trading of immovable property, which will take place under the same general principles, with the need to retain the form of a notarial act in such a situation. The draft provides for a revision of the article. 388 Civil Code. This is an article that refers to an institution of exploitation. The project provides for the addition of a fourth condition to the directory of indications relating to the lack of sufficient discernment. The lack of definitive discernment should be interpreted as a lack of knowledge necessary for responsible consideration of the purpose and effects of the conclusion of the proposed agreement. In the project we also grant the exploited party a choice of demand between reducing their benefits, increasing the provision due to it or cancelling the contract. It seems that since the good of this party has been affected by the defective conclusion of the contract, it should be her decision to continue to bind the agreement. Third amendment to Article 388 concerns the extension of the time limit for entitlement. The current two-year time limit for a court to request the formation of a legal relationship or the annulment of that agreement may be too short for it to be exploited In fact, it's a real possibility to report it. It is therefore proposed to extend that period so that it corresponds to the time limits laid down for the limitation of claims under Article. 118, which means that in the case of a contract concluded between traders, these requests will expire after 3 years, whereas in the case of contracts with the consumer, after 6 years. We also provide a presumption that, if the value of the benefit of one party exceeds at least twice the value of the mutual benefit, it is presumed that it exceeds it to a gross extent. In this way, we shift the burden of evidence to a professional entity. We believe that this entity is responsible for the sales technique, the preparation of the contractual model. Thus, the transfer of this burden of evidence is fully justified in a fair balance between the interests of the parties. We also propose that this provision be amended in substance, i.e. Article. 388 of the Civil Code was applied in a new version to contracts concluded after the date of entry into force of the Amending Act, which will allow all parties to prepare their legal relations accordingly, in particular as regards the examination of the level of discernment as to the appropriateness of the consequences of the conclusion of the contract or even the gross imbalance of benefits. We have also seen the need for legislative work on the regulation of the article. 688 with stamp 1 of the Civil Code. This involves seeking to ensure the protection of minors who are dependent on their parents by excluding their responsibility for payment of the rent and other charges due under the rental agreement. At present, this provision unfortunately has a different wording than the art standard. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 6 Housing Cooperatives Act. There, under joint and several responsibility, a dependent adult shall be released. We want this to be equalized also in the case of rental contracts. Of course, this exemption will also apply to adult dependent tenants who are not yet able to support themselves. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Please support the proposed project. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u423	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, please. High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the government bill amending the law - Code of Civil Procedure, print No 1344. The project concerns the introduction of solutions aimed at improving and clarifying the existing legislation in the Act - Civil Code on: interruption and suspension of limitation periods, the requirements of a notarial act for agricultural land intended to be part of an agricultural production cooperative, an institution for exploitation, the liability of adult tenants' children for the obligations of parents under a lease agreement. In the current state of law, both in the doctrine and in the case law of the general courts and in the Supreme Court, it should be considered dominant that the limitation period is interrupted as a result of inter alia. to submit a first request for a plea of settlement as an action taken before a court directly in order to investigate or establish or satisfy or safeguard it. On the other hand, there is controversy as to whether the same effect also applies further to the settlement or to the subsequent initiation of mediation. The proposed amendment will undoubtedly eliminate the practice for creditors to apply for a settlement attempt only in order to interrupt the limitation period, thereby reducing the number of cases affecting regional courts in this respect. It will also allow the procedural situation of the parties to the dispute to be clarified and will restore the request for a settlement attempt to its original proper purpose, namely the conclusion of a settlement, with a view to the legitimate interest of both the creditor and the debtor. With regard to out-of-court mediation proceedings, this change will undoubtedly increase the interest of this institution in the amicable settlement of disputes. This will enable the parties to focus their efforts on a genuine solution to conflicts and to reach an agreement by settlement. This solution seems to be optimal and in line with the expectations of both creditors and debtors, thus protecting the interests of both parties in the commitment relationship. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 159 The Civil Code excludes the requirement of a form of notarial act in situations where land contributed as a contribution to an agricultural production cooperative is to become the joint ownership of existing owners. The subject's literature draws attention to the low practical significance of this provision, as these situations in the current socio-economic realities are extremely rare, as are those in which the land is to become the property of the cooperative. This amendment will undoubtedly contribute to ensuring the certainty of the trading of immovable property, which will take place on a general basis, in the form of a notarial act, as set out in the provision of Article. 158 Civil Code. In the last decade, market practices have emerged in the legal field, involving contracts with similar effects to exploitation, but formally non-existent in the application of this institution. It is about encouraging older people, using marketing techniques, to acquire everyday moving goods at excessive prices. The sales techniques used make it difficult to offer prudently, which results in the conclusion of a sales contract under the influence of suggestions, without the need for consideration. There is also a problem with the so-called agreements. temporary loans granted by parabanking institutions to customers in situations - at least to a certain extent - forced. Another thing is the so-called practice. charges for securing loan contracts in which a low-value loan is secured by a transfer of property of several times higher value. There is also a phenomenon of the conclusion of franchise contracts which reserve disproportionately large benefits for franchisees and which are also concluded in a situation of incomplete knowledge of the franchisee of the actual value of the benefits offered in return by the franchisee. In these situations there are economic transfers resulting in extremely negative consequences for the exploited party, including execution and insolvency. Persons exploited because of other obstacles - the main financial but also health and educational information - are often unable to take advantage of their legal remedies. These phenomena must be assessed clearly negatively, not only for legal reasons - this is an unacceptable practice of using the weaker by the stronger - but also for social reasons. The reaction of the state to morally reprehensible practices should be as visible as possible. There is no doubt that the grounds (the bell) for the exploitation and the rights of the victim in such a situation are now set out far too narrowly. In view of the above, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I strongly recommend the adoption of the above-mentioned Law and Justice. a draft law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u425	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Codes are one of the most important elements of the current legal system after the Constitution. As a result, changes to the code rules should be highly prudent, since the codes constitute a set of rules which are, as it were, a basis for other solutions, a strong foundation for the legal system. Changes which take account of changing realities on the one hand and, on the other hand, lead to the elimination of provisions which in practice raise doubts often lead to different case law, which is always a difficult circumstance for the parties to participate in this procedure for fear of a decision which may be affected in the case in question. In this project we have changes regarding the effects of further requests for a settlement attempt. It is proposed that only the first request for a settlement trial should interrupt the limitation period. On the other hand, further requests to call for a settlement trial or to initiate mediation proceedings will result in suspension of the limitation period. The record proposed in this draft will have the effect of clearly regulating the effects of the first and subsequent requests for a settlement trial, but also contrary to the clearly emerging line of case law of the Supreme Court... It seems that the first call for a settlement trial will not be considered to be an abuse of procedural law, even if it is brought only to interrupt the limitation period without any intention to enter into a settlement or even to enter into settlements. The question therefore arises: Shouldn't there be an amendment to the limitation period after the first interruption of the limitation period, e.g. a shorter period, as indicated in the opinion of the Supreme Court. Substantial changes concern Article. 388 Civil Code, namely the issue of exploitation. In principle, four amendments to this project are proposed. Firstly, the definition of exploitation - as already pointed out by the Minister - is extended in relation to the lack of sufficient discernment. Secondly, there is a presumption that if the value of the benefit of one party exceeds the value of the mutual benefit twice, then we are talking about excess in a gross manner. Thirdly, the order of claims to be used is to be changed by sending the first cancellation of the contract to the plan. Finally, fourthly, the current deadline is to be converted into a limitation period, which will be longer than the deadline: 3 years for entrepreneurs and 6 years for consumers. With this solution, we must bear in mind, when setting longer deadlines, that setting too long deadlines may entail a risk of changing the value of benefits, including to the disadvantage of being exploited. Therefore, when setting these deadlines, when assessing these deadlines, we must also bear this in mind. The amendment will also apply to Article. 688 Finally, as I am running out of time, I would like to draw attention to one more point, namely during the proceedings before the committee or in plenary and during the discussion, we must also bear in mind the question of the relationship between the proposed changes to the content of the provisions already in force and the assessment... Thank you. ...these changes in the light of other existing provisions. This will need to be addressed during the procession process. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u427	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Left Club on the government draft law amending the Civil Code and the Civil Procedure Code, contained in print No. 1344. The main changes included in the submission are, above all, easier to challenge unfair contracts and more difficult interruption of the limitation period by the creditor. So much for the assumption. Following the introduction of an anti-crisis shield 3.0, the Ministry of Justice prepared a proposal for changes concerning, among others, the anti-Liquidity regulations. so-called. exploitation. This is a response to unfair market practices in the form of agreements with similar effects to exploitation effects but formally not in the scope currently applied in relation to this Article. 388 Civil Code. This is about all kinds of sale of pots, sheets or other household appliances with supposedly extraordinary parameters, while the only unique feature is the extremely high price. It is also a similar unfair practice to offer people under the so-called. a wall of relatively low loans at a high percentage on a pledge of much more valuable real estate or even an example of some franchise contracts, where the nature of mutual commitments deviates from balance like Jamaican runners. This is certainly an attempt to solve a legal problem and a rescue wheel for the victims of frauds. In situations such as those mentioned above, the weakness of the exploited party does not lie in its infirmity or inexperience as referred to in Article. 388 Civil Code, but lack of knowledge about the actual effects of the contract. Skillful presentation of a disadvantageous offer, calling on the addressee to believe that it is extremely attractive to believe that there is no need to verify before the contract is concluded, to use methods of manipulation, pressure, time pressure - all this makes it necessary to introduce such a provision. The project therefore provides for an extension of the grounds for exploitation to take advantage of the lack of sufficient discernment. Moreover, in the current legal situation, the party has 2 years to challenge the contract. This is the date of termination of any legitimate claim by the parties. Thanks to the proposed amendment in § 2 in Article. 388 of the Civil Code will be extended by one year and, where the party to the contract is a consumer - even four years. In addition, only a reduction or increase on the other side can be claimed at present. And only in cases where both would be excessively difficult can the contract be cancelled. Now it will depend on the injured party which of these three solutions will choose. Another important solution in the proposed proposal is the suspension of the limitation period for mediation and settlement talks. The proposed solution is based on the practice of the creditors of the institution to call for a settlement attempt, an institution which was practically intended to prevent the obligation from occurring. The solution proposed here is intended to eliminate this practice, the practice of creditors applying for a settlement attempt only in order to interrupt the limitation period. This is intended in theory to limit the number of cases affecting the regional courts. Question: "How effective?" In the event of a request for a settlement attempt and the initiation of mediation, the limitation period shall be suspended as appropriate for the duration of mediation or conciliation proceedings. However, a plea to settlement does not need to be specifically specified. It may seem that suspension is a good solution for both parties. It does not discourage the creditor from dealing amicably with the matter because it does not raise concerns about the expiry of the limitation period. However, this solution raises doubts because it still creates opportunities for abuse. In view of the above, I would like to say that the changes being treated seem to be expected and needed. Some of these changes... These changes affect the improvement of weaker actors, but at the same time we have some doubts about specific solutions. This submission requires amendments, it requires further work in the committee, so as a left club we will request that the project be sent to work in the committee. Thank you.

With regard to this draft law, both concerns are raised by representatives of economic environments, business circles, as well as by the scientific world. As far as business environments are concerned, the position of the Enterprise Council is indeed negative. It is stated, among others. the fact that the proposed amendments to the rules relating to the institution of exploitation and the interruption of the limitation period are further regulations striking the interests of the creditor and weakening its position vis-à-vis the debtor. The introduction of new rules to touch on concluded contracts by using exploitation institutions can have far-reaching consequences for business and the certainty of business turnover. This seeks to undermine the principle of compliance with contracts and the principle that ignorance of the law cannot be a reason to evade the effects of the agreements concluded and their obligations. The project envisages, among others, to provide a condition of non-recognition, to facilitate the exercise of the right in the form of cancellation of the contract, to provide that, if the value of the benefit of one party exceeds the value of the benefit of the other party twice, it is presumed that it exceeds it to a gross extent, and to introduce a longer period than at present to exercise the rights of the party invoking exploitation. In the view of the Enterprise Council, unfortunately, all these provisions, as proposed in the draft law, must be assessed clearly negatively. It should also be considered worrying, in particular, to introduce an easy possibility of cancellation of the contract, which will create a field for abuse in all areas of economic relations and may help unfair counterparties avoid the execution of contracts concluded and the payment of debts. The opinion of Professor Pisulinski at the Jagiellonian University is also negative, which in turn points out that calling for a settlement trial de lege summer does not interrupt the limitation period at all and the rules should not go in the direction that would allow it. Unlike the mediation that both parties have to agree to, only one party is requested to make a settlement attempt. The other side may not appear in court at all. Ba, she can sue the court herself to determine that she is not obliged to fulfil the benefit. The request for a settlement test therefore does not lead to a dispute. Professor Pisulinski asks: Why, then, is the creditor's unilateral legal action to interrupt the limitation period? In his opinion, there is no reason to reward someone who, having the opportunity to bring an action or refer a case to mediation, does not do so, for example. often tactically makes an impossible proposal for a settlement. Please note that the creditor may propose such terms of settlement which no reasonable opponent will accept and will receive a bonus in the form of interrupting the limitation period and counting that period again. The Professor also points out that the rules quite generally define the content of the call, and therefore in one case the extended arguments and settlement proposals may be given, and in the other case the call will contain a very general statement of facts and a proposal for a settlement, and yet each of them will meet the conditions. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! That's a few of those critical remarks. Our support for the submitted project, as far as the parliamentary club of the Polish Coalition is concerned, will also depend on the elimination of these shortcomings, which I have allowed to draw attention to. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u448	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law amending certain laws to improve the consistency of the terminology of the legal system is the response of the Parliament's Committee on Petitions to petitions addressed to the Sejm, which the committee considered to be justified. The analysis of the proposed amendments indicates that there are errors or errors in the laws to be amended as a result of amendments to other laws. Therefore, the introduction of these amendments will make it easier for the addressees of the amended acts to understand and apply them properly in the current normative environment. It should be stressed that the formulation of a separate law to remedy legislative and editorial errors and to adapt legal terminology to the current legal situation is correct and consistent with legislative technique. However, it is worth noting that if this law is passed, it will only be the second such act in our legal order. As indicated in the opinion sent by the Supreme Bar Council, the legislator also has other instruments to achieve the objective set by the projectors.
