ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u466	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The National Judicial Council is an independent and central state body whose functions are linked to the judicial authority. The powers of the National Judicial Council stem directly from the Constitution. And so with art. 179 of the Constitution shows that the National Judicial Council has exclusive competence to present proposals for the appointment of judges to the President of the Republic of Poland. And with art. Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. It follows from 2 of the Constitution that the Council also has the power to apply to the Constitutional Tribunal with a proposal on the compatibility with the Constitution of normative acts, in so far as they concern the independence of the courts and the independence of the judges. The specific competences of the Board are also laid down in Article. 3th Act on the National Judicial Council. The most important competences, which are most often carried out on an ongoing basis, most often at plenary meetings, include the examination of candidates submitted and the assessment of candidates to act as judges of the Supreme Court, administrative courts, common courts, the presentation to the President of applications for appointment of judges in these courts, the submission to the President of applications for the appointment of the examiners of the application for the posts of assistants, the opinion of draft normative acts, the adoption of resolutions on applications to the Constitutional Tribunal, and the supervision of the processing of personal data by the Supreme Judicial Authorities, such as the Constitutional Tribunal, the Court of State, the Supreme Court, the Supreme Administrative Court, appeals courts and military district courts, of course in proceedings conducted by those authorities. In carrying out these duties and prerogatives, the National Judicial Board held 16 plenary sessions in 2020 which lasted 59 days. 879 applications were identified to serve as judges. The Council adopted 912 resolutions on individual matters and presented proposals to the President of the Republic of Poland for the appointment of 489 persons to serve as judges in courts of various types and levels, including 293 women and 196 men. The teams of the National Judicial Council held 434 meetings, held 126 interviews with candidates. They also invited the President of the Court, the Head of the Department and the Visitor to the talks. The Council adopted 12 resolutions stating that there is no basis for visiting and illustrative activities, issued 66 opinions on draft normative acts and presented 9 positions on matters relating to the judiciary and judges. The National Judicial Council appealed to the Supreme Court against 13 unlawful judgments of the disciplinary courts of first instance. This calls for the Council to carry out 339 new competition proceedings in 2020, in which 348 free judicial posts were filled. It is worth noting that 1034 candidates took part in these competitions. The Council adopted 276 resolutions on the presentation to the President of proposals for the appointment of judges, including the nominations of 369 candidates were positive and 501 were negative. The Council also adopted 259 resolutions to terminate proceedings in connection with the withdrawal of applications for judicial positions by candidates. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The resolutions of the National Judicial Council are subject to appeal to the Supreme Court - the Chamber of Extraordinary Control and Public Affairs. A total of 102 appeals against 68 resolutions of the National Judicial Council were received in 2020, including 89 appeals concerning the appointment to serve as judges. The other appeals concerned other issues, such as the transfer to rest, resolutions on the further occupation of a judge of age, enabling him to move to rest and resolutions on the appeal against the division of the action. In 2020, 75 draft normative acts were received by the National Judicial Council. As I said, the Council issued 66 opinions on these files. As regards the structure of these projects, this was as follows. It was 51 draft laws, including 13 government projects, 25 parliamentary projects, 12 senate projects and one civic bill. Opinions were also issued on 24 regulations, including 23 regulations addressed to the Council by the Minister of Justice and one draft regulation by the President of the Republic of Poland. Ladies and gentlemen! Within the framework of the National Council of Courts, there are committees which prepare draft resolutions on matters which are subject to the competence of those committees. Thus, the Commission for Complaints, Requests and petitions, which investigates complaints relating to the activities of appeals courts, disciplinary actions, as well as the Chairman of the National Judicial Board, recognised 542 letters in 2020. The Commission for Visitation and Appreciation, which prepares draft resolutions on the conduct of the court's visits or organisational unit of the court, the magistrate's magistrate's vestment or vestibule, recognised 252 letters. The Committee on Professional Ethics of Judges and Assessors, preparing draft resolutions on a set of rules on Professional Ethics of Judges and Assessors, recognised in 2020. 62 writings. The National Judicial Council also operates a Commission on disciplinary liability of judges and court assessors. Its task is to examine disciplinary judgments, to submit proposals to the board for disciplinary action, to challenge disciplinary decisions. The Commission recognised 225 letters in 2020. Following the amendment of the Law on General Courts and the delegation of the National Council of Courts to supervise the processing of personal data by the Constitutional Tribunal, the Supreme Court, the Supreme Administrative Court in 2019 was established within the Board of the Commission for the supervision of the processing of personal data. In 2020, this committee considered reports from supervisors on the implementation of legal protection tasks. She has established cooperation with the President of the Office for Personal Data Protection concerning inter alia. the separation of competences between these authorities. It has developed a regulation on the detailed procedure of its work and the working procedures of the Council at plenary meetings on personal data breaches. There is also a Commission for judicial reform in the National Judicial Council. In 2020, she selected six teams that dealt with issues such as the selection model of judges, the judges of peace, the model of disciplinary action, the status of judge and the structure of the judiciary. In addition, the validity of the so-called. flattening the structure of the judiciary. The Council also had a library committee. This Commission was liquidated in May this year and no longer operates. In connection with the work of the committees operating within the National Judicial Council in the media area, there have been issues relating to the costs of the operation of the National Judicial Council. In a follow-up speech, the Supreme Audit Chamber indicated that expenditure on committees selected and functioning within the Council increased to 122 000 in 2020. zł. However, expenditure on the functioning of the Council, and in particular on the diet of the members of the Council, should be considered in a broader context. It was on July 16th, on the day of the birth of a man who hardly anyone deserves to be called a hero, a hero of three nations: Polish, Jewish and Hungarian. This monument is to become a symbolic place that integrates people, showing the values for which our hero gave his life: patriotism, courage, nobility, love of freedom and belief that every man is important regardless of religion, place of birth or social status. Henryk Sławik came from an unrich, multi-children's family and graduated from only a universal school, and in his history he was a prominent social, local and political activist and an active participant in the three Silesian uprisings. In his business he placed Poland and Polishness first. It is not possible in such a short speech to approximate all the achievements of this outstanding Pole. However, it is worth recalling some of the most important events in his biography, which show a huge effort of a man, who has gained a leading position in the social life of the region and the country, until he has finally become a hero saving thousands of lives. In 1928 Henryk Sławik became the editor-in-chief of the Labour Newspaper, a press agency in Silesia. He enjoyed great trust in the environment, which for many years entrusted him with the position of President of the Syndicate of Polish Journalists Silesia and Dąbrowski. At the same time he co-organized the Association of Workers' Universitys and Workers' Sports Clubs. In September 1939, he arrived in Hungary. He became President of the Civic Committee for the Care of Polish Refugees. Thanks to their cooperation with József Antall, senior representative of the Hungarian government for war refugees, they jointly rescued several thousand Poles of Jewish nationality. Sławik was arrested by the Gestapo in 1944. Tortured during the investigation did not release his Hungarian companion, which saved Antall's life. Henry Sławik died in the Mauthausen-Gusen extermination camp. For many years the character and achievements of the hero of Wideness were doomed to oblivion. Fortunately, in many places of Poland and the world he was restored an honorable place in history, among others. thanks to the procedures of Polish Ambassador Grzegorz Łubczyk, and today, you can say Ambassador Henryk Sławik, for which I thank him warmly from this place. The year of Henryk Sławicz in Silesia was declared 2014. Henry Sławicz for his integrity to Jerusalem's Yad Vashem Institute honored him with the title of Righteous Among the Nations. He was posthumously awarded the Commander's Cross with the Star of the Order of Rebirth of Poland. A permanent exhibition dedicated to the life and activities of Sławik has been available in the City Centre of Culture in Jastrzębie-Zdrój for years. Elementary school number 18 in Wideka bears his name. Today, at the place where it all began, at the monument, we can all worship Henry Sławik, the hero of Szeroka, so that this outstanding figure will never be forgotten again, and the next generation will be reminded that man is always the most important, regardless of social status, religion or place of birth. I believe (the bell) that a character as unusual as Henry Sławik should find its permanent place on history books. This from this place, the Sejm of the Republic, I appeal to the Prime Minister and to the Minister of Education. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u508	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The European Green Deal obliges our country to make many changes aimed at improving the quality of life on our planet and changing our country to greener. The objectives set by the European Commission are very ambitious and touch on many aspects of our economy. By 2030, we need to halve the use of the most dangerous pesticides. By the same year, we are obliged to reduce fertilization by 20% or increase the size of our organic farms so that by 2030 they represent 1/4 of all crops in our country. However, the biggest challenge of the European Green Deal is to make our country climate neutral by 2050. This is a very big problem for us as a country whose energy is largely based on coal. One of the solutions to this pressing problem is to increase the share of renewable energy sources in our energy mix. The good news is that Poles are increasingly reaching for domestic photovoltaic installations, and this market is growing from year to year. Currently, according to various studies, as much as 75% households in single-family homes want to invest in photovoltaics. This gives 3.75 million households with around 14 million inhabitants. In addition, at the end of April 2021, the installed photovoltaics in Poland amounted to 4.7 MW. This means an increase of 129% for April 2020 This increase is largely the result of micro-installations. Unfortunately, the rules that they plan to introduce can make it more difficult and discourage citizens from starting photovoltaics. At the Parliamentary Group meeting. The zero-emission economy and the European Green Deal have been discussed on the proposal to change the prosumer system by abandoning the discharge system since 1 January 2022. In the current model, the prosumer benefits around 50 gr/kWh, which the system accounts for. In a new model where it will have the right to self-consumption and sale of energy, unfortunately this financial advantage will fall to around 24 gr/kWh. This means that the benefits for the prosumer will fall by half. In the case of standard installations with a capacity of 6,5 KW, this means that the night financial benefits for the prosumer will decrease by about 1300 PLN. The return on investment will also be extended from 6.5 years to 10 years. Although these changes are now proposals and not existing laws, this has already caused a huge stir in the photovoltaics market. There is a strong interest in photovoltaic installations in 2021, which is unfavorable. This will translate into a large number of installation of many low-quality components made by people with low competence. This gives rise to another problem of installation fires. With regard to fire regulations, it should be mentioned that the Construction Law does not require a building permit or a declaration of construction works consisting in installing a photovoltaic micro-installation with an electrical capacity of up to 50 kW. In addition, in the case of micro-installations with a power of less than 6,5 kW, and the average installation in our country has a power of 6 kW, it does not impose an obligation to agree the design of such a micro-installation with the fire protection assessor and to report the fact that it is installed in the authorities of the State Fire Department. Therefore, most installations are controlled by the owner, and the possible failure and its consequences depend on the professionalism of the installer company. As you can see, legislative proposals, first of all, will discourage citizens from installing photovoltaics due to increasing costs for consumers, and secondly, they can make roof fires more frequent. The Association of Fotovoltaic Industries Poland PV has negatively assessed the proposed changes. You should support the development of small home installations, and the development of home energy storage facilities is also necessary. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Polish photovoltaics are increasingly developed, but further sustainable development will only be possible with energy storage. Increasing consumer costs is a step in the opposite direction. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u512	Dear Marshal! Gentlemen! On July 24, we will celebrate the anniversary of the establishment of the State Police. Recovering independence in November 1918 was the beginning of unification of Polish lands in many aspects. Especially important were those that dealt with external and internal security issues. In fact, all the boundaries of that time were on fire. The young Polish state had to fight for its presence on the map of Europe despite the formally declared independence. Changes had to be made quickly in the structure of the widely understood police authorities, which were active in the three former partitions in order to effectively secure the backup of the fighting fronts. The process of the formation of the State Police was long and complicated, and the date of 24 July 1919 could be regarded as its beginning, when the State Police were established on the former lands of the former Russian occupation, eliminating the previously created Communist Police and the People's Militia. Next, police organisations were gradually introduced into the newly created service, usually set up as a model for the existing police security authorities. It took a long time to eliminate all kinds of nuances and discrepancies, and attempts to amend the so-called. Police laws have been taken many times. Most historians accept the Regulation of the President of the Republic of Poland of 6 March 1928 as the end of the unification and reorganisation process. Polish police in the interwar period were well trained and equipped. She was very active in Interpol activities. It is worth quoting one of the studies: For 21 years of its existence, the State Police have earned a reputation for a high-level professional and extremely well-managed organization. Some researchers dealing with the history of police formations indicate that the organization of security bodies in Portugal and Japan was created on the designs developed in Poland. Police officers sacrificed blood in the September 1939 campaign, many officers gave their lives in the fight against three aggressors. In areas occupied by the Soviets, the repression was not only themselves, but also their families, exported to inhuman soil. About 12 thousand were taken into Soviet captivity. Officers, of which over 6,000. was murdered in the spring of 1940. They were previously held in camp in Ostaszkow, and the crimes were committed in Tver. When in the early 1990s, just after political change, the Russians allowed archaeological work at police graves, after removing several layers of sand from the eyes of the observing people, a shocking view appeared. The earth was a blue-grained color within a few meters of the excavation from the uniforms of the policemen who were murdered and buried there. We remember them because of their ethos our state police has grown out of today's ethos. On the occasion of the Police Day, I give the officers of all levels, especially from the Piotrków and Belchatów districts, the best wishes. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The German criminal was transferred to Poland by Americans. The trial began before the Provincial Court for M.S. On July 18, 1951, Warsaw was sentenced to death and public rights. The indictment took place among others. a murder charge of more than 56,000. persons, belonging to the SS criminal organization, giving orders to shoot 100 Poles and mass murders. The perverse fate caused him and Stroop to be in a cell - arrested by Communists - Kazimierz Moczarski, a participant of the Warsaw Uprising. The result of these conversations was recognized and appreciated in the historical environment of the book "Conversations with the executioner." The death sentence was executed by hanging in a Mokotów prison in Warsaw on 6 March 1952.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u519	On July 13, 1944, the Red Army won Vilnius with an important part of the National Army. In the city began the legal activity of representatives of the Polish underground administration, in Vilnius and around the city there were concentrated several thousand AK soldiers from the Vilnius and Novgorod districts. Commandant Wolf reported to the chief chief in London: Politycości of the city is beating in the eye. Polish-Soviet talks began on the creation of the Polish corps fighting on the Eastern front with AK soldiers. On July 17, the Soviets insidiously arrested the commander Wolf. On the same day, in the Podwilen village of Bogusze, AK officers from the commanding staff were arrested and arrived there at the invitation of the Soviets. Polish guerrilla troops began marching towards Rudnicka Forest in order to avoid liquidation by the Soviets. On the way they were disarmed and distracted by forces of the NKVD and the Red Army. The mass arrests of the AK soldiers have begun. Stopped in the number of about 6 thousand. They were imprisoned in a provisional camp in Medniki and then transported to Kaluga. Many of them did not return to Poland anymore, many of them returned only after many years. Respect and memory fighting for free Poland.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u520	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Next Sunday, on July 25th, the National Safety Day will be held again. This is an extremely important social action, which requires us to lean even further on the safety problem of road users. On this special day, following the voice of the organizers of the action, I would like to appeal to the ladies and gentlemen of Members, and through you to all the inhabitants of Poland, for careful and prudent driving every day, so that we can all travel safely on Polish roads. According to police statistics, the number of accidents is decreasing every year. In 2007 there were almost 50,000, last year - more than 23 thousand. A similar trend is seen when we look at the number of deaths: 5583 in 2007 and over 2400 last year. Unfortunately, the main causes of accidents remain the same: the failure to give priority to the journey and excessive speed. In addition, statistics on the number of road users affected by alcohol are alarming. In 2020, road users, managers, pedestrians and drunk passengers participated in 2540 road accidents, 10.8% the total number of deaths of 327 people. Let the next edition of Safe Driver's Day be the best opportunity to draw attention to the need to improve safety on Polish roads. I would like to call on the Prime Minister and Minister Adamczyk to speed up the implementation of road investments. Statistics clearly show that improving the quality of infrastructure affects road users' safety. It is therefore necessary to implement as quickly as possible all planned investments aimed at improving safety. It is impossible not to mention drivers here, addressing all countries directly. I would like to make a heartfelt appeal for consideration, especially during the holiday period, to ensure that special caution is exercised. I want all those who contribute to improving safety, thank and wish perseverance and consequences. The best wish for you should be that on July 25th, on Safe Driver's Day, there should be no accident on Polish roads. I am fully convinced that this is up to all of us and that through special mobilisation we will be able to achieve our goal.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u521	I would like to present a statement by the Association of May 77 on the article by Wojciech Czuchnowski, we prefer to believe in legend. How the myth of the martyr's death of Pyjas (Gazet Wyborcza from 22 May 2021) and the publication of the Pyjas case under the editorial board of Maria Anna Potocka (Publishing House Bunkier, 2021) was dissolved: The text of Wojciech Czuchnowski, published in the Wyborcza Gazeta. We prefer to believe in legend. As the myth of Pyjas' martyrdom unfolded, we find it unreliable and inconclusive. We won't argue with him or correct his mistakes. Except for one thing: it is not true that the teams of experts appointed by the D.A.'s Office in 1977 considered the death of Staszek Pyjas to have fallen down the stairs. On the contrary. On 21 May, a local vision was carried out on behalf of the prosecutor's office with the participation of experts. In the preserved document from the vision we can read that the fugitives went to reality at ul. Szewska 7 to get acquainted with the place of the body and the area of the entire stairwell. As a result of the local vision, the experts decided that it was necessary to exclude the fall of the deceased from any floor (through the staircase rail) on the stone floor. Wojciech Czuchnowski based his thesis on the publications cited in the article. Around the Pyjas case, which appeared under the editorial of Maria Anna Potocka (Publishing house Bunkier, 2021) as a kind of catalogue for the exhibition Dorota Nieznalska (the case of Stanisław Pyjas), presented in the Bunker of Art in 2020, as well as on the published article by Mrs Potocka, three characters, three dramas, in which the author, being the director of the Museum of Contemporary Art of the MOCAK and the Gallery of Contemporary Art of the Bunker of Art, unexpectedly decided to test herself as a historian, investigative officer, and even a colleague of Pyjas. Since we are not aware of Mrs Potocka's qualifications to explain the causes of death of anyone, especially Stanisław Pyjas, we can only suspect that the purpose of the publication is to dissuade Pyjas and the student community of the Solidarity Committee, as well as to whitewash Lesław Maleszka (secret cooperation of SB ps. "Ketman"), which, in Potocka's opinion, did not harm anyone. We don't agree with that! It's hard to minimize. e.g. cooperation with the Security Service in writing anonymously calling for dealing with Pyjas. I'm interested in creating myths. I was attracted to snooping for the truth, fascinated by lying the truth for the sake of defending the myth - explains his attitude towards the exhibition by the director of Bunkra Sztuki and MOCAK. The psychology of people poorly known or unknown and dead by dilettants is a risky procedure, but Potocka (a specialist in contemporary art) does not avoid it. According to our experience, the greatest determination in such operations is shown by those who, before or after the death of Pyjas, did not put themselves in any danger of power. By judging this situation according to their own moral barometers, they gain justification for their passiveness. According to red. W. Chuchnowski Maria Anna Potocka knew Pyjas and his group. None of us remember her from that time, either as Potocka or under Socha's maiden name. Nor is it true in the text given by W. Czuchnowski that Maria Anna Potocka was the author of the exhibition Stanisław Pyjas. The author and curator of the exhibition was Dorota Nieznalska, whose position in this matter we attach. We are not afraid of questions or even more of seeking answers to them, including those concerning the death of Stanisław Pyjas, but we cannot agree to the misrepresentation, manipulation of facts and quotations, to the relativization of the criminal activity of the Peerellian political police - the Security Service and the harmful role played in the fight against opponents of the system of her secret co-workers. The purpose of the Association of May 77 in 2002 is to ensure that the truth about the times of the People's Republic of Poland is passed on, and in the name of this truth we cannot agree to its violation. Attached is Dorota Nieznalska's statement: Dear Ladies and Gentlemen! As the author of the idea, realizer and curator of the exhibitions: Stanisław Pyjas's case at the European Solidarity Centre in Gdańsk in 2019 and at the Art Bunker Gallery in Krakow in 2020 I took with great anger the publishing house "Pyjas" edited by Maria Anna Potocka. The publication, which in some way was to complement the exhibition free of political propaganda, or attempt to formulate its own content, cast a voice to friends, friends of Stanisław Pyjas, to complete his image or to emphasize the memory of him, the environment and those dramatic times, became a pretext for forcing Mrs Potocka's own views and not in line with the facts of the case of his death. I am outraged by such nonchalant treatment of the authors of texts, actually shouting them (it is about setting the proportion of texts in the publishing house) and devalueing the history of the young oppositionist, Krakow's environment, which cares about this memory. I am angry and ashamed that I trusted Director Potocka because I realize that through the presented exhibitions I caused a memory of Stanisław Pyjas, about a poet, idealist, ambitious young humanist, opponent, who ultimately, according to the director - drunk died in an accident! It is obvious that everyone has the right to their own opinion, but not every version so-called. its own truth should be published, thus taking over and dominating the space of memory, as is the case in the publication "Pyjas" referred to here. Respectfully, Dorota Unknown.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-22-sejm-35-2.u522	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! In today's parliamentary statement, I wanted to draw attention to the risks associated with the finalization of Nord Stream 2 investments. It is 1,200 km of a two-thread bus leading from Russia to Germany through the Baltic Sea, which is to transport 55 billion m3 of natural gas per year. After an effective completion of the investment, Russia will get its hands on a strong instrument for further sharing transatlantic interests and using its weapons against Ukraine and Belarus, acting as it is customary, both openly and secretly. Poland recognises the threat of further marching Russia into Ukraine, as the existing pipeline system running across Ukraine will become redundant shortly after the completion and launch of Nord Stream 2. The continuation of Nord Stream 2 is a clear violation by Germany of European solidarity in the area of EU energy policy. We are aware that Germany is pursuing its interests and that their interests coincide for various reasons with Russian interests, but are also in conflict with the interests of the countries of Central Eastern Europe. Poland is very disappointed by the withdrawal from some sanctions of the current US administration towards Nord Stream 2. The change in Donald Trump's policy by the current President Biden is based on false hopes of restoring relations with the EU. The German Government officially supports Nord Stream 2, claiming that the project is of a business rather than political nature. This cannot be accepted because German companies Uniper and Wintershall and French England, Austrian OMV and the British-Dutch Royal Dutch Shell participate in the financing of the project. We are witnessing Russia's victory when it comes to building this pipeline. The Russian Federation will have a significant impact on the European gas market. Former German Chancellor Gerhard Schröder was active in Russian energy companies. After the expiry of his term of office, he was appointed Head of the Board of Shareholders of Nord Stream AG. In October 2016, Schröder headed the company's board of directors. Nord Stream 2 AG. A year later, he was nominated as an independent director on the board of Rosnieft, where according to German journalists he could earn about 600 thousand. Euros per year. A number of other people connected with the German SPD also went to Russian energy. An example of such cooperation is Henning Voscherau, who in 2012 became the head of the Board of Directors of South Stream Transport AG. In October 2016, Marion Scheller, an influential official of the German Ministry of Economy, passed to Nord Stream 2 AG. Also Karin Kneissl, former Austrian Foreign Minister, was nominated for the board of directors of the Russian Rosnieft Group. She was also the head of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and was famous for the invitation of Vladimir Putin to her wedding. So it is wise and wise to continue the strategy of sleep. Lech Kaczyński by the Government of the United Right assuming diversification of gas sources through the construction of gasport in Świnoujście and construction of the Baltic Pipe pipeline.
